## Checkpoint296 — Unbounded auxiliary mass and the polar metric

Example 15.6.8 on printed page 457 constructs a compact complex sixfold with a smooth strictly positive genuinely nonclosed background and unbounded bounded-potential mass. Two projective lines, the Hopf threefold and one further projective line form the product. A positive nonconstant coefficient h over the first two curves makes the vertical term nonclosed. Scaling the Hopf smooth potentials by c=min h preserves positivity; top-degree expansion gives the lower bound 120c^3 times their diverging threefold masses. The factor 120=6!/3! is explicit. The separated unit-divisor locus has a complete geodesic polar metric of diameter 4, closed ideal analytic closure and unique nearest-point 1-Lipschitz ideal retraction. Two analytic ideal models have polar distance 1, while every ordinary representative of each has zero extended mass. The product locus is explicit; the unrestricted topology problem is unchanged.

## Checkpoint294 — General auxiliary product models

Proposition 15.6.18 on page455 proves the following. Arbitrary compact complex auxiliary factors and smooth semipositive vertical terms preserve the separated curve envelope identities, complete geodesic polar metric, ideal analytic closure and nearest-point ideal retraction without an ambient mass bound. Zero-obstacle equality, atomic polar curvature, relative-envelope membership and equality at every smooth obstacle remain equivalent. All model comparison volumes vanish. Compact-fiber descent and closed top-degree auxiliary test forms prove the nonbig cases; smooth curve gauges prove bigness, and the mixed derivative component proves genuine nonclosedness.

## Aim and conventions

Develop a self-contained compact Hermitian counterpart of Mingchen Xia's *Singularities in global pluripotential theory*, retaining the supplied template and style. The main narrative must work without assuming bounded Monge–Ampère mass or positive lower mass throughout. Gauduchon metrics, Monge–Ampère equations and connections with non-Kähler geometry belong alongside singularity comparison, envelopes, I-goodness, traces and partial Bergman kernels.

Use standard theorem environments, upright differential d and the standard multiplier-ideal envelope notation. Distinguish proved statements, precise external inputs, conditional results and open problems. Before using any result of Popovici, consult the supplied private working reference and independently check the entire proof and dependencies. That private reference is not book content or package material.

## Structure

The book opens with a mathematical Introduction comparing the Hermitian and Kähler mechanisms. A generated subject index follows the bibliography; maintain its definition and result locators as the book develops.

The book has 33 active chapters and an appendix. Chapter 1 collects general psh foundations: compactness, regularization, integrability, local capacity tools, multiplier ideals, divisorial estimates, general envelopes and descent. Later chapters use internal references to these results. Chapters 1–28 form the main mathematical sequence. Part IX separates the substantial mass-dependent theory:

- Chapter 29, *Volumes under bounded mass*: finite singularity volumes, comparison, relative full mass and the conditional ideal-envelope volume characterization.
- Chapter 30, *Relative equations under positive lower mass*: semipositive big backgrounds and smaller-background model references, without a blanket bounded mass assumption.
- Chapter 31, *Big backgrounds under bounded mass*: arbitrary smooth big backgrounds, bounded mass and a model reference of positive lower volume.

Chapter 32 collects research questions; Chapter 33 records history and paper sources. Citations of books may remain in the mathematical body, while non-book citations and exact locators belong to the final historical chapter. Under the author's 27 September instruction, manually supplied authorless papers are integrated without citations or bibliography entries, and their original files are not backed up to GitHub. This is an attribution policy, not permission to remove proof dependencies or weaken hypotheses. The build checks citation placement. The relocation and second-pass scope are recorded in `research/editorial/psh-foundations-history-review-112.md` and the two accompanying maps.

The former standalone analytic-approximation chapter is absorbed into Chapter 1, including its restriction corollary. The temporarily excluded *A transcendental Nakamaye theorem* remains hidden, with its three files and prior history preserved. Bott–Chern loci and LCK geometry are Chapters 27–28. Earlier checkpoint narratives below retain their original numbering; `research/READING-GUIDE.md` gives the current route.

## Current proof boundary

Checkpoint85 reorganized the exposition without changing the retained results. Checkpoint86 broadens the elementary relative-envelope theory: stretching, nonempty finite rooftops and equality of ordinary singularity envelopes hold for any smooth background and genuine reference. Their proofs use convex combinations, the upper-envelope theorem and normalized L1 compactness. Model-reference positivity and the infinite-rooftop and equation arguments retain their stated additional assumptions. Chapter 32 reuses the general convex proofs without weakening its mass hypotheses. Checkpoint87 places the existing arbitrary-background weighted lower-semicontinuity proof in Chapter 13 and removes its Chapter 32 duplicate. The proof allows infinite mass; the monotone-limit prerequisites and the later equation hypotheses remain explicit. The earlier systematic pass and its limits are in `research/editorial/chapter-review-20260921.md`; 180 bounded verifier reports retain their individual accepted inputs and scopes. Release checks are recorded separately and are not whole-book mathematical certification.

Upper bounded mass and positive lower mass are independent. The elliptic example has a balanced metric with finite upper mass and zero lower mass. Extended non-pluripolar measures, finite damping and envelopes therefore provide the general framework. A finite linear Gauduchon trace cannot be substituted for nonlinear mass integrability. Nor does representing a measure as an integrable density against a Monge–Ampère measure solve an equation for that measure.

For a Hermitian background, positive lower mass characterizes normalized solvability for every finite non-pluripolar measure. A fixed L1 density instead has an attained subsolution threshold with an exact Gauduchon formula. Under positive lower mass, this threshold is the equation scalar, and normalized greatest exponential solutions converge in capacity along their L1-convergent subsequences. Without that mass property, uniform first moments for one maximizing sequence and a genuine smaller-background envelope for the greatest normalized threshold subsolution give the corresponding exact equation. Root tails alone are not shown to supply these additional hypotheses.

Smooth strictly positive dual attainment, in dimension at least two, gives a smooth exact threshold equation, uniqueness and uniform convergence of the normalized exponential family. A smooth semipositive maximizer positive off a proper analytic set gives exactness, local smoothness, uniqueness modulo constants, and the stated trace/current convergence estimates. It does not automatically give global regularity or the smaller-background envelope needed for capacity convergence. Integrable-current attainment by itself gives no such regularity. These distinctions remain central to Chapter 26.

The normalization functionals in Chapter 14 have the established entropy comparisons, reference monotonicity and dual formulas. Relative positive-form bounds now compare these functionals uniformly over all bounded tests; ordinary divisorial removal gives the same comparison for positive residual backgrounds. Supporting densities have the stated weak L1 stability, while full-potential convergence also controls moving sections and their normalization constants. On the admitted closed model domain the functional retains exactly mass. Common-residual examples in both closed and nonclosed backgrounds show that its pseudometric need not separate singularity types. Chapter 15 keeps ordinary and ideal envelopes distinct. Chapter 18 identifies the source topology on positive-mass curve ideal envelopes with total variation of their atomic data; a fixed positive lower mass gives completeness and analytic density, but not total boundedness. This does not construct the general Hermitian singularity metric.

Linear current continuity, exceptional-divisor formulas and a canonical ordinary type on one model do not imply nonlinear volume continuity or higher-dimensional trace realization on all models. The explicit elliptic current/envelope/section descent, boundary-volume calculations and primitive elliptic bundles are test cases for these questions. The K3 example in Chapter 33 has trivial canonical bundle, a balanced metric of finite upper mass, no divisors and only vertical curves; its unrestricted lower mass remains unresolved here. The historical draft suggestion about current descent is unproved and outside the book.

Checkpoint88 integrates the supplied incomplete-metric example in Section 28.8 after direct and bounded Rethlas review. Its unique finite-energy solution has minimal singularities but is incomplete throughout the ample locus. The class is big and non-nef; completeness under nefness and flow compatibility remains separate. Chapter 33 records this boundary.

Checkpoint89 adds interior smoothness in Section 13.20 for locally Holder minimal-singularity solutions of arbitrary smooth big backgrounds. The derivative argument has been fully checked under this explicit premise. The supplied statement with only a locally smooth positive density had a missing Holder input, preserved in the adverse Rethlas report. Global Lp, p>1, supplies that premise through Dang26, which remains an explicit additional input rather than a proof reconstructed here. The local theorem uses neither metric mass property. Regularity at the pole set, at density zeros, for nonminimal types, or from local smoothness alone remains outside its scope.

Checkpoint90 reconstructs the Holder input rather than importing it. Sections 13.20–13.21 prove one-sided stability uniformly over references between a fixed strict barrier and zero, compactness of the fixed-reference family with uniformly Lp-bounded densities, and local Holder continuity for minimal-singularity solutions. The linear curvature error for a smooth nonclosed lower form is derived from Demailly’s classical expansion using a fixed full decomposable curvature bound and a frozen quadratic on the averaging support. Section 13.22 now derives its global Lp smoothness corollary entirely from these internal results. The global integrability condition remains essential to the stated proof; no conclusion is asserted from local smoothness alone, for arbitrary nonminimal regularity, or across poles. The focused review is recorded in `research/editorial/big-reference-stability-holder-review.md`.

Checkpoint91 moves stability to Section 13.19 and uses its compactness theorem to shorten the normalized minimal-equation proof in Section 13.20. A single lemma supplies uniform type and scalar bounds for all positive exponents at most one. Subsection 13.21.1 identifies every zero-exponent cluster equation, proves whole-family scalar and strong Lp density convergence, and obtains local Holder subsequence convergence. Whole-family potential convergence remains conditional on uniqueness within the stated invariant class. No metric mass property is imposed. The exact factoring and bounded review are in `research/editorial/big-exponential-compactness-review.md`.

Checkpoint91 also integrates the updated Archive regularity proofs in Section 13.22: the Bellman/ABP strong-to-viscosity passage, explicit Chern curvature identities, Dirichlet density conversion and the one-dimensional reduction. All existing statements retain their hypotheses. The separate correct focused report does not validate the broader local-density-only source claim; the original adverse report is unchanged. The selective bibliography merge and final proof boundary are recorded in `research/editorial/nonclosed-archive-integration-review.md`.

Checkpoint92 develops the compact closure of normalized smooth Gauduchon tests in Section 4.4, its exact linear threshold and polar characterization, and stability under uniform changes of the background. The zero-degree lemma consolidates smooth-attainment proofs. Chapters 26 and 28 distinguish this closure from all positive dd^c-closed currents: the latter test smooth positivity and nefness, and the exceptional curve gives a strict difference between the two thresholds. The nonlinear determinant duality retains its entire current domain and separate proof. No mass property or regularity of a minimizing current follows from the linear compactness. See `research/editorial/gauduchon-linear-duality-review.md` for the bounded verification and second-pass scope.

Checkpoint93 integrates the supplied arbitrary-big-class ample-locus smoothness theorem as Theorem 28.3.2, without nefness. Its global smooth positive volume form satisfies the global Lp condition in the internally reconstructed Dang theorem, Section 13.21. The full proof chain includes stability, regularization, local Holder continuity and the derivative bootstrap; classical BEGZ existence and minimal-singularity estimates remain explicit standard inputs. The scalar/local-equation formulation is justified using the analytic complement's zero mass. The broader local-density-only intermediate source claim is not adopted. No completeness or extension across the non-Kähler locus is asserted. The source comparison, bounded full-chain verification and precise remaining scope are recorded in `research/editorial/ample-locus-integration-review.md`.

Checkpoint94 completes the requested local-notation revision across every active chapter, nested proof input, appendix and front matter. Each section declares or precisely recalls its objects and hypotheses before use, and proofs introduce their own temporary symbols. Two manual passes cover all 188 active author files. The same rule and coverage audit are incorporated into mingchen-writing. The companion note is synchronized with its shared book sections and its own local declarations. This changes exposition rather than the proof boundary: general and mass-dependent regimes retain their assumptions. ## Growing vanishing orders, checkpoint96

Section 27.12 now completes the exact one-variable degenerate weighted-kernel model with a vanishing-order-uniform Poisson comparison, a logarithmic envelope limit, locally total-variation kernel-measure convergence and a Gaussian transition for arbitrary growing vanishing orders. The limiting kernel measure discards the pole atom of the envelope's ordinary current. The full audit's adverse endpoint sentence at r=1 is preserved and corrected in a separate successful repair audit; see `research/editorial/degenerate-growing-kernels-review-96.md`. This is a noncompact model, not a compact Hermitian localization theorem or a novelty claim. No new arXiv theorem or mass property is a premise. The earlier fixed-jet transfer note remains independently unaudited.

Problem 33.12.3 specifies the first compact-curve localization question and the growth range making the local weight remainder negligible. Uniform global norm/extension estimates and extensions through non-Kähler fibrations remain open. Chapter 2 has completed this cycle's first/second proof-and-writing pass. Continue with Chapter 3; the broad general-theory and daily reading obligations remain active.

## Geometric normalization stability, checkpoint167

The supplied q8-2 manuscript is integrated in Sections 14.8–14.10. Relative positive-form bounds compare the entire normalization functional uniformly over all bounded tests. ## Exact traces on Kahler submanifolds, checkpoint188

Chapter 18 now realizes every trace order on every smooth compact modification when the target submanifold is Kahler. The ambient manifold and its background may be non-Kahler and nonclosed. A sufficiently large closed target background gives positive model mass, so classical closed model continuity supplies the upper divisorial bound. Every larger Hermitian background is then exact by envelope order. This does not establish exactness in an arbitrary original restricted background or ordinary-type independence under arbitrary background changes. All eighteen prior Chapter 18 formal statements remain; one theorem/proof and one label are added. Problem 32.8.1 is narrowed to non-Kahler targets.

## Introduction and subject index, checkpoint209

The whole-book Introduction now explains how closedness links mass, comparison and energy in Kähler theory, and how nonclosed backgrounds separate these mechanisms. It distinguishes the ambient geometry from the chosen background, mass conservation from an energy primitive, trace measures from their conserved total mass, scalar uniqueness from potential uniqueness, and ideal-envelope approximation from ordinary singularity topology. It concludes with the roles of traces, sections, modifications and a concise reading route.

A four-page subject index follows the bibliography and appears in the contents. Its 246 entries comprise 242 clickable page locators and four alternate-term cross-references, placed at definitions, results and examples in 113 author inputs. The supplied indexing typography is retained; a book-specific style keeps a main heading with its first subentry. The mathematical body and its proof boundary are unchanged. See `research/editorial/introduction-and-index-209.md`.


## Divisorial currents and intrinsic trace envelopes, checkpoint210

Chapter 22 now uses the intrinsic trace envelope as the canonical common majorant on every modification. The opening distinguishes divisorial currents visible on one model from the full trace inequalities on all models. The common-majorant proof uses Chapter 18 instead of repeating its compactness argument. The constant-trace representative proof uses the normalized Green inverse from Chapter 5, with its top-degree datum and normalization explicit. The exceptional-divisor discussion gives the residual-current identity in the closed-background case and explains why its correction is compatible with exact pullback of the full trace envelope. The complete four-input revision preserves the all-model realization boundary and closed-background scope of residual Bott–Chern classes.
